# Foundation finish series: ledger

This is the progress record for the [foundation finish series](README.md). **Every session must update it**, including a session that only investigates, gets blocked, or changes nothing.

## How to update this ledger

1. **Before your first edit**, set your document's row in the status board to `in progress` and fill in the date.
2. **Before you commit**, append one entry to the session log using the template at the end of this file. Entries are append-only: never rewrite or delete an earlier entry. To correct one, add a new entry that names the entry it corrects.
3. **In the same change**, update the status board row: status, last commit, date, and the next action.
4. Add any new blocker, question, deferred row, or unverified assumption to its section below. When one is resolved, mark it resolved with the date and the entry that resolved it. Do not delete it.
5. Update the checkpoint table in the [milestone ledger](../../verification/milestone-1.md#checkpoint-status-and-acceptance-criteria) when a checkpoint's status changes, and link the entry here. Packet evidence for this series is written here, not there.
6. Keep entries factual. Record commands as they were run and results as they were printed. A check that did not run is listed as unexecuted with its reason.

Status values: `not started`, `in progress`, `blocked` (name the blocker), `done`, `deferred` (owner decision; name the missing resource).

A document is `done` only when every acceptance case in it has recorded evidence, its gates pass on the committed tree, and its exit condition in the [plan](../foundation-completion-flash.md) is met. Otherwise it stays `in progress` or `blocked`.

## CI pace optimisation — owner-directed prerequisite to Session B

- **Status:** done for the pre-Session-B implementation and available PR/local handoff (2026-10-23). Full-code verification passed at `facf409`; the documentation-only checkpoint `6e73cc4` passed Foundation `39880666037` in 39s and Security `39880665805` in 31s, reusing executed evidence from `facf409` while refreshing format/npm audit/Trivy. The owner requested this finalisation and a saved [Session B launch prompt](session-b-prompt.md); it is substantive handoff work, not a result-only commit loop. Default-branch native/cache-maintenance entry points, current Intel macOS native setup, minimum-OS/package rows and end-to-end release qualification remain unexecuted in their carried rows. This closes the pre-session handoff only, not those qualification rows. Document 05 subsequently recorded Linux native evidence on 2026-10-23; its browser/bounds follow-up is reopened on 2026-10-25 above.
- **Acceptance:** bounded authenticated Ubuntu provisioning with an official-mirror fallback; fail-closed change-aware checks backed by earlier passing evidence; bounded dependency caches; one renderer unit/coverage execution; no duplicate desktop frontend build; release assets may build in parallel but cannot publish before all qualification passes; FLASH workers have an explicit checkpoint/evidence workflow.
- **Verification:** regression tests for scope, malformed/missing evidence, cache retention and provisioning failures; pinned actionlint and shellcheck; renderer checks/build/browser flows; hosted code and documentation runs; native evidence remains separately required. Record unexecuted environments explicitly.

## Unverified assumptions

Statements the code or a decision record relies on that only a live run can confirm. The owner document must confirm or refute each one and record which.

| Assumption                                                                                                                         | Source        | To be checked by                                   |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| A frozen worker makes the liveness read wait for its 15-second deadline; stale within 45 s, `TALOS_UNAVAILABLE` within about 92 s  | Decision 0013 | Document 01                                        |
| An idle healthy stream stays current for longer than the 30-second window                                                          | Decision 0013 | Document 01                                        |
| A suspend released at the stale marker recovers under the next generation                                                          | Decision 0013 | Document 01                                        |
| The official client's watch retry keeps a broken connection silent to the helper, and a rebooted node rejects the earlier bookmark | Decision 0013 | Document 01                                        |
| A reboot that outlasts the stale bound plus the budget ends `TALOS_UNAVAILABLE`                                                    | Decision 0013 | Document 01                                        |
| Power loss and partition behave like a freeze                                                                                      | Decision 0013 | Unassigned; no fixture action is designed for them |

- **Document:** none (series maintenance, before document 01).

- **Status after this session:** no document status changed. Blockers B1 and B2 are resolved; B3 is fixed locally and awaits its hosted confirmation.
- **Acceptance cases:** none.
- **What changed:**
  - `helper/cmd/talos-pilot-helper/main_test.go`: `TestProbeCancellationDuringBackoffEndsCleanly` now uses the injected fake clock and waits for the pending 30-second backoff before it cancels. No production code changed.
  - Series README and documents 04, 07, and 08: `gh` is available; the conditional wording about unreadable hosted results no longer names B2.
- **Commands and results** (openSUSE Tumbleweed x86_64, Go 1.27.2):
  - `gh auth status`: signed in as `agiledevel`. `gh run list --branch ft-foundation-completion`: runs 38299801331 (`b662b1a`) and 38300140517 (`481421a`).
  - Both runs, read while still in progress: `renderer` success; `desktop (windows-2022)` failure in the step "Verify Go helper source and modules", `--- FAIL: TestProbeCancellationDuringBackoffEndsCleanly`, "unexpected frame after cancellation" with an error frame; `desktop (ubuntu-22.04)`, `desktop (macos-15)`, and `desktop (macos-15-intel)` still in progress when read.
  - Before the fix, `go test -run 'TestProbeCancellationDuringBackoffEndsCleanly$' -count=3000 ./cmd/talos-pilot-helper/`: 51 failures, so the race is not specific to Windows.
  - After the fix: the same command with `-count=5000` passes; `-count=2000 -cpu=1,4` passes; `-race -count=300` passes; the whole package with `-count=300` passes; the whole package with `-race -count=40` passes.
  - `gofmt -l .` prints nothing; `go vet ./...`, `go build ./...`, `go test -count=1 ./...`, and `go test -race -count=1 ./...` pass.
  - `pnpm format:check`, `git diff --check`, and the local link and anchor check pass.
- **Evidence class:** deterministic tests and hosted CI results only. No native run and no real cluster.
- **Fixture:** none.
- **Failed or unexecuted:**
  - The hosted Ubuntu and macOS desktop jobs of both runs were not read to completion. They are in progress, not passed.
  - The hosted run for this entry's commit has not been read. Until it is, the Windows fix is confirmed locally only.
- **Review findings:**
  - The test cancelled as soon as it read the stale marker. The stale marker is sent before the first reconnect attempt, so the cancellation could cross that attempt's failure. The helper then reports the failure instead of a clean cancelled end, which is its documented disposition for a terminal outcome that races a cancellation. The test was wrong, not the helper.
  - The test dates from commit `f9d56a8`. No hosted run had executed it, because pushes had failed since `c310fbd`. The F1a handoff's stress runs covered only the `TestStall` tests.
  - The carried row "Live cancellation during an in-flight reconnect attempt or backoff" is related: a cancellation that lands during an attempt can end with that attempt's failure. That is existing behavior and is unchanged here.
- **Assumptions confirmed or refuted:** none.
- **Questions and blockers raised:** B3.
- **Next:** read the hosted result for this entry's commit, all five jobs, and record it in the next entry. If the Windows job still fails, fix that before document 01.

A failed delivery attachment retains its spawned task handle for abort-and-join. The packet-wide task/socket/permit/data baseline matrix, including shutdown while a watch is active, remains open.
- **Acceptance cases:** 10 synthetic full-queue termination returns queued bytes to zero after drain; native closed-channel callback is detected through the five-second acknowledgement timeout and session closes; queue byte overflow by projected bytes remains separately covered by the existing per-message bounded accounting rather than a dedicated synthetic saturation run. 11 passed synthetically for cancellation during initial paginated list, 410 relist pagination, and transient retry backoff; each joins within 250 ms, releases queued and retained bytes, and produces no subsequent API requests. 12 native session-close joins an active watch; a closed renderer callback ends by acknowledgement timeout; app shutdown while a watch is active is not independently observed. 13 remains incomplete as a full acceptance-case matrix. 14 native ordered delivery, consumer-close timeout, and session-close cleanup passed; an underlying Tauri `Channel.send` failure and full application shutdown with a live stream remain unobserved. Other acceptance cases retain evidence from earlier entries, including the post-transport live case 15.
- **What changed:** native test unregisters its real Tauri channel callback after receiving the first event and asserts the timeout terminal; added queue-full and list/relist/backoff cancellation tests with resource-counter assertions; delivery attachment now takes a mutable optional JoinHandle, preserving ownership on failure so the caller aborts and joins it; added a regression test for that failed-attachment ownership contract.
- **Commands and results:** `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked kubernetes:: -- --nocapture` passed (47 tests); default and `native-test` `cargo test --locked --all-targets` passed (131 passed, 1 ignored in each library suite; integration/session suites passed; live fixture cases stay ignored by default); default and `native-test` Clippy passed with `-D warnings`; `pnpm test:native` passed on openSUSE Tumbleweed x86_64 / WebKitGTK 605.1.15 (12 helper-status and 7 Kubernetes tests, including the 5.7-second acknowledgement timeout); `pnpm check` passed (18 files, 151 tests and Vite production build); `cargo test --locked --doc`, `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --locked --no-deps`, `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --check`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm contracts:check`, `pnpm inventory:check`, `pnpm desktop:build`, `pnpm production:check`, and `git diff --check` passed.

No new live fixture run was needed for these failure-path changes; the immediately preceding entry records live 1.36.5 watch recovery after the transport change.

First compiles of new tests caught test-only `Ordering` imports, which were corrected before the successful run. No required final gate failed. The complete per-case task/socket/permit/projection baseline matrix, active-watch application shutdown, and direct Tauri send failure case remain unexecuted.
- **Review findings:** review found that a failed `attach_watch_delivery` previously dropped a `JoinHandle`, detaching the delivery task. Passing a mutable option makes ownership transfer explicit only on successful registration; the command aborts and awaits the handle on failure, and the unit test verifies the handle remains available. The renderer callback closure is distinct from the Rust channel transport closing, so that native case proves acknowledgement-timeout cleanup but does not claim `ChannelClosed` classification.

### 2026-10-22 — Session A2, CI tiers (decision 0016)

- **Document:** none. This is Session A2 of the [close-out plan](closeout-plan.md), implementing [decision 0016](../../decisions/0016-ci-test-tiering.md). No packet status changed.
- **Session:** DeepSeek Flash; base revision `9ec5bf2`; final revision: the commit carrying this entry.
- **Status after this session:** implemented. Required `Foundation checks` no longer runs `pnpm test:native`; a new `Native tests` workflow runs it on the four desktop runners via manual dispatch, a weekly schedule, and a release call; release qualification calls it for the exact revision; the required-check Cargo cache now keys its prune/save on its own last build; the native workflow owns a separate cache. Workflow lint passed locally. The hosted required-CI run, the native dispatch, the deterministic-defect probe, and the cache observations are recorded in the follow-up entry.
- **Acceptance cases:** none. This session implements a CI tier decision; it changes no product behavior.
- **What changed:**
  - `.github/workflows/native.yml`: new reusable/manual/scheduled `Native tests` workflow. `workflow_call` takes a required `revision`, `workflow_dispatch` takes an optional revision/ref, and a weekly schedule runs Mondays 03:23 UTC. It runs the four-runner matrix (`ubuntu-22.04`, `macos-15`, `macos-15-intel`, `windows-2022`), uses the pinned Node/pnpm/Rust/Go/protoc toolchain, runs `pnpm test:native`, uploads a per-runner log artifact with 14-day retention, and keeps `contents: read`. It owns a `native-v1` Cargo cache, pruning first-party artifacts before a save that only happens after the release executable exists.
  - `.github/workflows/check.yml`: removed the `Run actual Tauri IPC tests` step; rewrote the cache-prune/save condition to key on `src-tauri/target/release/talos-pilot*` (the executable `pnpm desktop:build` produces) instead of the native release example, with the comments updated. `xvfb` stays in the Linux prerequisites because `pnpm production:check` launches the packaged app through `xvfb-run` when no display is set.
  - `.github/workflows/release.yml`: new `native` job calling `native.yml` with `needs.source.outputs.revision`; `assets` and `draft` now depend on it.
  - `docs/ci-release.md` and `README.md`: the workflow is described as implemented, with its triggers, four-runner matrix, separate cache, evidence artifact, read-only permissions, and its status as a merge and release gate rather than a branch-protection check. The required-cache paragraph now describes the new save condition.
  - This ledger: this entry and the later hosted/evidence follow-up.
- **Commands and results** (openSUSE Tumbleweed x86_64; Node 26.12.1, pnpm 12.15.0, Rust 1.99.1, Go 1.27.3, protoc 36.2): the pinned actionlint 1.7.12 archive (SHA-256 `8aca8db96f1b94770f1b0d72b6dddcb1ebb8123cb3712530b08cc387b349a3d8`, matched against the digest in `check.yml`) was downloaded and `actionlint` over the repository exited 0, covering the new and edited workflows including the reusable call. `pnpm format:check` and `git diff --check` passed. No application gate was rerun because no application source changed; the hosted foundation run that exercises the edited workflow is the applicable verification.
- **Evidence class:** workflow lint and documentation only. No native run, fixture, or hosted result yet in this entry.
- **Fixture:** none. No host or cluster state changed.
- **Failed or unexecuted:** the required-CI run for this commit, the four-runner native dispatch, the required-check failure probe on a deliberate defect, the cache save/hit observations, and any end-to-end release call are recorded in the follow-up entry. A real release rehearsal is not executed because it needs a version tag, and tagging or releasing is outside the owner's standing permission.
- **Review findings:** the desktop job still needs `xvfb` for `pnpm production:check`, so the package stays even though the native suite moved. The cache condition now matches the executable every desktop runner builds, including `talos-pilot.exe` on Windows, and the native workflow's own cache cannot collide with `cargo-v1`. The native test script already builds its own native-test application and synthetic API example, so no extra build step precedes it. The four-runner matrix and the 90-minute bound match the desktop job's known worst case with a bounded margin.
- **Assumptions confirmed or refuted:** none yet; the hosted runs confirm or refute them in the follow-up.
- **Questions or blockers raised:** none.
- **Next:** push, read the required-CI result to confirm it passes without the native step, dispatch `native.yml` for the same revision on `ft-foundation-completion`, run the deterministic-defect probe on a scratch branch and delete it, record the cache entries, then write the follow-up entry.

## Entry template

Copy this block, fill every field, and append it to the session log. Write `none` where a field does not apply; do not delete fields.

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD — document NN, short title

- **Document:** NN, and the packet or step worked on.
- **Session:** the model that did the work; base revision; final revision (the commit carrying this entry).
- **Status after this session:** not started / in progress / blocked / done, and why.
- **Acceptance cases:** each case from the document, marked passed, failed, or unexecuted.
- **What changed:** files, contracts, and decision records, one line each.
- **Commands and results:** each command as run, with its result. Name the host and the toolchain versions.
- **Evidence class:** which results are deterministic tests, which are native Tauri runs, and which are real-cluster runs.
- **Fixture:** identity, versions, and topology if one was used; the baseline taken before provisioning; the restoration evidence afterwards. Write `none` if no fixture was touched.
- **Failed or unexecuted:** each check that failed or did not run, with the reason and what it limits.
- **Review findings:** what the separate review pass found and how each finding was resolved.
- **Assumptions confirmed or refuted:** rows of the unverified-assumptions table this session settled.
- **Questions and blockers raised:** IDs added to the tables above.
- **Next:** the first thing the next session should do.
```

The earlier unrelated flaky storage test (`concurrent_initialization_serializes_the_first_migration`, SQLite `DatabaseBusy`) passed in that run and in 25/25 local repetitions. Security [40210048580](https://github.com/agiledevel/talos-pilot/actions/runs/40210048580) passed.
- **Failure-artifact contract:** the renderer job runs the flows with `DEBUG=vite:deps,vite:optimize-deps,vite:hmr`, keeps the full output in the job log and copies at most 2 MiB to `test-results/vite-evidence.log`; on failure it uploads `test-results/` as `browser-failure-evidence` with 7-day retention. The existing `playwright.config.ts` trace retention and `tests/e2e/browser.ts` attachments supply the trace, the ≤256 × 2,048-character lifecycle record and the ≤256-record keyboard/focus timeline. Verified with a temporary probe that reloaded the page and failed an assertion: the artifact held `trace.zip`, `error-context.md`, both navigations plus `browser-evidence: document reloaded` in `browser-lifecycle`, a `browser-input` attachment and `vite-evidence.log`, and the CI shell block returned the suite's nonzero status. The probe was deleted before the final gates; the workflow passes pinned actionlint 1.7.12 with ShellCheck 0.11.0.
- **Controlled comparison** (`playwright.preview.config.ts`, `pnpm test:e2e:preview`, diagnostic only: `vite preview` serves `dist/` on the same origin and port with the same scripted IPC transport):

  | Variant                             | Iterations                         | Result                                  | Reload signals | Vite `deps`/`optimize-deps` lines |
  | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------- | --------------------------------- |
  | Development server, cold cache      | 2 workers × `--repeat-each=2` (32) | 32 passed                               | 0              | 265                               |
  | Development server, warm cache      | 2 workers × `--repeat-each=2` (32) | 32 passed                               | 0              | 28                                |
  | Production preview, before F6-B12   | 2 workers × `--repeat-each=2` (32) | 30 passed, 2 failed (eager editor load) | 0              | 0                                 |
  | Production preview, after F6-B12    | 2 workers × `--repeat-each=2` (32) | 32 passed                               | 0              | 0                                 |
  | Menu focus only, development server | 2 workers × `--repeat-each=8` (8)  | 8 passed                                | 0              | 24                                |
  | Menu focus only, production preview | 2 workers × `--repeat-each=8` (8)  | 8 passed                                | 0              | 0                                 |

- **F6-B12 (new, corrected):** the preview run failed the editor lazy-load assertion because the built document had already requested the Monaco chunk. `dist/index.html` carried `<link rel="modulepreload" href="/assets/editor-monaco-*.js">` plus the editor stylesheet, and the entry chunk began `import{a as e}from"./editor-monaco-*.js"`. The `build.rollupOptions.output.manualChunks` rule put Monaco in a named chunk that also captured Vite's shared preload helper, so the entry statically depended on the 2.7 MB chunk and prefetched it and its 99 kB CSS at startup. The development server cannot expose that graph, and the earlier runtime/first-use evidence only proved the chunk was not executed before use. Mechanism checks were actual builds: removing the rule produced a clean document with no static chunk import; `modulePreload: false` and an explicit helper chunk name did not. Correction: the `manualChunks` rule is removed and Vite's default dynamic-import chunking owns the boundary; `pnpm production:check` gained `assertShellStaysLazy()`, which fails on a `modulepreload` link or a `YamlEditorSurface` reference in `dist/index.html`. The eager build fails it with "The production shell preloads a static chunk"; the corrected build passes. The lazy chunk is now `YamlEditorSurface-*.js` (2,744.62 kB / 709.64 kB gzip, Monaco included) and the shell document preloads only its entry script and stylesheet. The [F6 evidence](../../verification/f6-editor-workers-csp.md) records the corrected sizes, the F6-B12 mechanism and a dated correction naming case 5.
- **Menu focus, investigated separately:** not reproduced. The keyboard View-menu test passed 8/8 on the development server and 8/8 on the production preview at hosted-like concurrency, plus twice in every full-suite variant, with zero reload signals in all four logs. The original failures still have no explained cause, so S0 does not close; the new hosted artifacts make the next real failure decidable.
- **Additional reload, investigated separately:** not reproduced. The production preview produced no reload in 64 full executions (32 before and 32 after the F6-B12 correction) and 8 menu-only executions, which rules out an application-level reload in these flows; the earlier reloads were seen only through the development server and remain unattributed. The cold development run started from an empty project-local `node_modules/.vite` and still reloaded nothing while Vite logged 265 optimizer lines, so the recorded prebundle correction is present and no late discovery occurred.
- **Commands and results on the final tree** (Linux development host; Node 26.12.1, pnpm 12.15.0, Rust 1.99.1, Go 1.27.3, Linux WebKitGTK 605.1.15): `pnpm check:ci` passed (format, type-aware Oxlint including the new configs, zero Svelte errors/warnings, 191 tests in 26 files, production build); `pnpm test:e2e` 16 passed; `pnpm test:e2e:preview --workers=2 --repeat-each=2` 32 passed after the correction (30 before); `pnpm test:native` under private Xvfb and XDG directories 23 passed across 3 spec files with no helper process or synthetic endpoint left; `pnpm desktop:build`, `pnpm production:check` (including the new lazy-shell check) and `pnpm advisories:check` (67,079 symbols, 5 recorded advisories) passed; `pnpm inventory:check` (535 npm, 614 cargo, 143 Go modules), `pnpm contracts:check`, `pnpm format:check` and `git diff --check` passed; pinned `actionlint` with ShellCheck passed on the changed workflow.

The preview comparison is diagnostic; it is not a packaging, CSP, offline, installed-package, macOS/Windows or native claim. No cluster, fixture, host network/firewall, VM, container or credential was touched.

The artifact step keeps failure propagation and bounds the uploaded log at 2 MiB with 7-day retention; no assertion, timeout, concurrency or mock boundary changed; the preview config is additive and documented as diagnostic; the build correction removes an option instead of adding one, and its guard fails on the previously committed behavior and passes on the corrected output; no CSP, capability, credential or production test surface changed.

These are reused-entry results for the squash source, not this S1 branch and not native/fixture/installed-package evidence.
- **Acceptance cases for S1 (all required before document 05 closes):**
  1. The existing 64 KiB contract is an explicit UTF-8 **byte** ceiling (65,536 bytes), accepted exactly at the limit and rejected at limit+1 for ASCII, 2-byte and 4-byte text. The check is allocation-conscious and runs before model creation, model replacement, worker transfer and diff-model creation.
  2. Initial, replaced, edited/pasted/undo-redo documents are bounded in the editor; both diff sides are bounded atomically as a pair. A rejected input is visible, the prior accepted document/model remains usable, the text is never silently truncated, and rejected text is not retained as a model or worker message. A rejected initial document creates no model/worker and remains recoverable when a bounded replacement arrives.
  3. Validation allows one in-flight request and at most one coalesced latest bounded document. Debounce delays dispatch but cannot build an unbounded task/message queue. A document change invalidates the visible diagnostics immediately, before the next dispatch; stale, duplicate or out-of-order responses cannot publish for newer text.
  4. Failure semantics are explicit and bounded: a 5 s worker timeout or a worker error publishes a safe failure diagnostic, clears in-flight/queued work, terminates the stalled worker and does not auto-retry the same document; the next edit may start a fresh bounded request. Disposal cancels timers, drops queued work, ignores late responses and releases worker/listener/model ownership.
  5. Regression tests observe dispatched/in-flight/queued counts and actual resource counters, not only that a cleanup callback or terminate spy ran. The native suite must show the changed bounds and rejection/cleanup behavior plus the existing worker diagnostic, diff, offline/same-origin assets, CSP denial, zero remote requests and model/worker/listener return to baseline.
  6. Decision 0019, this packet, the F6 evidence, status documents and this ledger are updated in the same change. C6/M1, B11, F7 and later packets stay open.
- **Contracts/ownership:** `yamlEditorSession.svelte.ts` keeps one model/editor/worker/observer set; the new bounded validation queue owns one debounce timer, one in-flight timeout, one latest pending document and request ids; `yamlDiffSession.svelte.ts` keeps two read-only models. Worker-request runtime validation remains defense in depth. The feasibility surface gains user-visible oversized-draft controls; no test-only IPC, command, capability or mock bypass is added.
- **Final gate plan:** narrow Vitest while iterating; separate final `pnpm check:ci` and `pnpm test:e2e`; actual Linux `pnpm test:native` under private Xvfb/XDG directories; then, in order, `pnpm desktop:build`, `pnpm production:check`, `pnpm advisories:check`; documentation link/format/whitespace checks; separate final diff review; hosted results read after the S1 PR exists. No Rust/Go/contract source changes are planned, so those unit/contract gates are not rerun without a changed input; the native test build compiles the Rust application.
- **Next:** implement the bounds and queue, tests, native evidence, documentation and review. Document 05 remains **in progress** until every row above is evidenced on the final tree.

- **Acceptance cases for F7 (all required before document 06 closes):** F7-01 terminal byte/line ceilings at and beyond each limit, including newest partial/multibyte/CRLF suffix; F7-02 oversize single line/burst admission without retaining the source, control rejection, empty clear, evicted text absent after replay; F7-03 slow/stalled writer under rapid replacement within active/latest/chunk limits, with stale callbacks and the 5 s deadline unable to revive a session; F7-04 chart 8 × 2,000 aligned values, newest-window eviction, ninth-series/malformed rejection preserving prior data, empty-then-valid recovery; F7-05 resize/root+OS theme/density before, during and after close without errors, data loss or live resources, plus idempotent cleanup and zero-size ignores; F7-06 independent lazy loading, close-while-loading, stale success/failure and reopen; F7-07 local CSS/assets only with no remote request and inline/unauthorized-origin scripts blocked under the packaged CSP with real library rendering; F7-08 named synthetic surfaces, keyboard operation/exit/focus return, chart numeric equivalent, no motion under reduced motion and the fixed shell layout at 1280×800, 1024×700 and 720×480. Evidence tiers stay separate: deterministic/injected, browser mock, actual Linux Tauri/WebKitGTK.
- **Selected terminal geometry, cell and queue bounds (recorded before terminal code):** visible geometry is clamped to **20–200 columns** and **5–50 rows**; emulator scrollback is **1,000 rows**, so the emulator retains at most **200 × (50 + 1,000) = 210,000 cells**. Admitted transcript stays within **1,048,576 UTF-8 bytes and 10,000 logical lines** at once. Replay owns at most one admitted snapshot (≤ 1 MiB), one latest pending snapshot (≤ 1 MiB) and one in-flight library write of **≤ 32 KiB canonical text, ≤ 64 KiB after CR/LF rendering expansion**; the stall deadline is **5,000 ms** with no automatic retry. These are retained-data/queue limits, not a whole-process heap or emulator-cell-memory claim.
- **Chart bounds:** at most **8 value series** (time excluded) × **2,000 newest aligned samples**, copied into independent arrays (2000 × 9 Float64 values = 144,000 bytes for a full snapshot), and at most **80 Unicode code points** per series ID and label. No animation and no optional auto-producer.
- **Contracts/ownership planned:** one synchronous typed snapshot reader plus a revision per surface, generating oversize proposals on demand and retaining only admitted data or a generation intent; pure admission/eviction modules tested before library integration; one attachment per surface owning library instance, observers/listeners, pending frame, deadline and idempotent teardown; a shared cancellable lazy-surface boundary extracted only for demonstrated reuse; one appearance adapter resolving canvas-compatible token colours and observing root attributes plus the OS colour-scheme query. No CSP, capability, IPC, credential, logging or persistence change is planned.
- **Gate plan:** narrow Vitest while iterating; then the final tree's `pnpm check:ci` and normal `pnpm test:e2e`; actual Linux `pnpm test:native` under private Xvfb/XDG directories with the explicit `-screen 0 1280x800x24` fixture; then, in order, `pnpm desktop:build`, `pnpm production:check`, `pnpm advisories:check`; frozen install, inventory generate/check for the new pins, `pnpm audit --audit-level=low`, `pnpm audit:go` and the current Cargo audit/advisory applicability and license review; a bounded native keyboard/rendered visual pass by trusted OS input (xdotool XTEST on the owned private display), naming that method; documentation link/format/whitespace checks; and a separate final diff review with affected gates rerun after fixes. ### 2026-10-25 — document 06, S2-B terminal wrapper and replay session

- **Scope:** S2-B of [document 06](06-f7-terminal-chart.md) on `docs/s2-flash-readiness`, continuing from S2-A `f40cc61`. Adds the read-only synthetic terminal: a lazily imported Home surface, a typed snapshot reader plus revision, bounded admission, callback-paced replay with reset/replacement coalescing and a five-second stall deadline, appearance/resize ownership, and explicit teardown. No chart, F8, fixture, package, budget or M2 work; no CSP, capability, IPC, credential or dependency change beyond the S2-A pins.
- **Shared helpers extracted for demonstrated reuse:** the cancellable lazy-surface boundary moved from the editor owner to `src/lib/state/lazySurface.svelte.ts` (four consumers now, with its moved tests); a specialized-view diagnostics/ownership module counts instances, listeners, observers, pending frame, replay deadline, retained/pending bytes and in-flight write separately for the terminal and chart; one appearance adapter maps `data-mode`/`data-density` plus the OS colour-scheme query onto canvas-compatible colours through the existing Skeleton tokens. The editor keeps its own errors/counters and the existing editor suites are unchanged.
- **Terminal contract as implemented:** CRLF is folded before accounting; unsupported C0/DEL/C1 controls are rejected while the accepted transcript is preserved; the newest suffix is retained within 1,048,576 UTF-8 bytes and 10,000 logical lines, with a Unicode-safe newest suffix for one oversized line; empty clears. The replay session owns one admitted snapshot, one latest pending snapshot, and one rendered write whose encoded length stays at or below 64 KiB (canonical chunks at or below 32 KiB, code-point aligned) awaiting its real callback. A newer replacement resets and replays at the next callback; duplicate/late callbacks and post-disposal work are inert; the 5 s deadline fails visibly and drops pending data without automatic retry. Geometry is clamped to 20–200 columns and 5–50 visible rows with 1,000 scrollback rows (210,000-cell bound); diagnostics report bounded first/newest non-empty emulator rows and the emulator row count.
- **Verification:** `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:svelte` (zero errors/warnings), focused Vitest (`src/features/terminal`, `src/lib/state`, `src/lib/format`: 15 files/101 tests), and the new `tests/e2e/specialized-views.spec.ts` terminal flows passed against the real pinned xterm.js in Chromium: lazy first use (no xterm request before opening, no Monaco request after), rendered transcript and bounded emulator inspection, both ceilings plus one oversized line and a burst, rejection preserving accepted output, clear and recovery, theme/density/resize cycles at three viewport sizes, axe scans, keyboard focus/scroll and focus return, and baseline counters after close. Production build succeeds with the terminal in its own `TerminalSurface` chunk and zero `xterm` occurrences in the initial `index` chunk.
- **Limits/next:** browser-mock and deterministic evidence only so far; the actual Linux Tauri/WebKitGTK packaged-CSP terminal rows, injected-stall behavior, and the native keyboard pass remain S2-D. The chart surface (S2-C) is next in this session.

### 2026-10-25 — document 06, S2-C chart wrapper and accessible summary

- **Scope:** S2-C of [document 06](06-f7-terminal-chart.md), continuing from S2-B `09987dd`. Adds the synthetic aligned chart: a separately lazily imported Home surface, synchronous proposal admission, one coalesced render frame with one retained plus one latest bounded window, in-place same-shape `setData` updates, instance recreation when the series shape or appearance changes, clamped sizing, and explicit teardown. No F8/fixture/package/budget/M2 work; no CSP, capability, IPC, credential or dependency change.
- **Chart contract as implemented:** at most 8 value series (time excluded) and the newest 2,000 aligned samples; retained timestamps must be finite and strictly increasing and retained values finite; a ninth series, duplicate or >80-code-point ID/label, misaligned length, or non-finite retained value rejects the whole replacement and preserves the accepted chart; an extra sample evicts one aligned row; empty releases the plotted data and a later valid proposal recovers without reopen. The admitted window is copied into independent `Float64Array`s (2,000 × 9 × 8 = 144,000 bytes for a full window); the owner retains only the requested kind and sequence. Diagnostics expose instance/observer/frame counts, rendered and scheduled bytes and the coalescing slot depth.
- **Accessibility/appearance:** the canvas host is decorative to assistive technology and is paired with a bounded numeric equivalent: a native disclosure containing one row per series with latest, minimum and maximum, at most eight rows and no hidden full data set. The single appearance adapter maps root `data-mode`/`data-density` and the OS colour-scheme query onto canvas-compatible colours; because uPlot compiles axis/grid colours and fonts into accessors, a theme or density change rebuilds the one instance from the retained window instead of mutating private compiled state. No animation and no automatic producer exist.
- **Verification:** `pnpm lint` and `pnpm lint:svelte` pass; focused Vitest for `src/features/charts`, `src/lib/state` and `src/features/terminal` passes (chart rendering projections, samples, admission, surface state, owner state). The extended `tests/e2e/specialized-views.spec.ts` passes **7/7** against the real pinned uPlot and xterm.js in Chromium: independent lazy first use (opening the chart requests no xterm/Monaco/terminal asset and Home requests neither library), real canvas pixel painting, 8 × 2,000 rendering, sample-limit eviction, ninth-series/length/non-finite/label rejection preserving the chart, clear and recovery, theme/density/resize cycles at three sizes, disclosure keyboard operation, axe scans, reduced-motion/no-animation checks, and zero instance/observer/frame/byte counters after close. Production build emits a separate `ChartSurface` chunk (59.05 kB / 25.50 kB gzip) and `TerminalSurface` chunk (341.26 kB / 86.99 kB gzip) with zero `uplot`/`xterm` occurrences in the initial `index` chunk.
- **Limits/next:** browser-mock and deterministic evidence only. Actual Linux Tauri/WebKitGTK rendering, packaged-CSP/offline denial, native keyboard pass, production rebuild/exclusion/advisory checks, hosted CI and the separate final review are S2-D. S2-D is next in this session.

### 2026-10-26 — document 08, S4-A review corrections: verifier format completeness and versioned libraries

- **Document:** [08/F9](../foundation-finish/08-f9-linux-packages.md), checkpoint S4-A review. F9 stays **in progress**; no acceptance row is claimed.
- **Corrects:** the S4-A implementation entry above. An independent review of that diff found two defects; this entry records the defect, the correction and the re-run gates. The review's reproductions were synthetic.
- **Findings and fixes:**
  - **Downloaded-artifact verification accepted an incomplete Linux format set.** `scripts/verify-artifacts.ts` validated the target and then trusted the provenance's installer list, so a directory with only a synthetic DEB and matching checksums exited `Verified 1 targets and 1 installers`. `scripts/lib/artifacts.ts` now exports `requireInstallerFormats(target, installers)`, called before checksum and digest validation: each installer must be target-prefixed, must name a declared extension for that target, and each declared extension must appear exactly once (Linux DEB/RPM/AppImage, macOS DMG, Windows NSIS). A foreign-target name, an undeclared format, or an empty/duplicated set fails.
  - **The payload gate misclassified versioned shared libraries as programs.** `isProgram` excluded `.so`, `.dylib` and `.dll` only, so an executable `libglib-2.0.so.0.8600.0` was rejected as undeclared. The suffix rule now accepts `so`, `so.<digits>[.<digits>…]`, `dylib` and `dll`; arbitrary programs and library-looking names with other suffixes (`libnotreally.so.debug`) still fail.
- **Regressions:** `scripts/lib/artifacts.test.ts` covers complete, missing, duplicated, foreign-target and undeclared-format sets for Linux, macOS and Windows; `scripts/release-assets.test.ts` reproduces the incomplete, duplicated and foreign sets through the `pnpm release:verify` CLI and keeps a complete Linux set passing; `scripts/lib/payload.test.ts` adds positive versioned-library coverage (`libglib-2.0.so.0.8600.0`, `libfoo.so.1`, `libbar.so`, versioned `.dylib`) and keeps the undeclared-program negatives.
- **Re-run on the final tree:** `pnpm exec vitest run scripts` → **151 tests / 19 files** (`/tmp/tp-s4a-r1-scripts.log`); `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `git diff --check` and the pinned actionlint/ShellCheck pass; `pnpm check:ci` → **394 tests / 49 files**, coverage 71.37 % statements / 74.17 % branches, `svelte-check` 0/0, production build (`/tmp/tp-s4a-r1-check-ci.log`); `pnpm test:e2e` → **31/31** in 26.5 s (`/tmp/tp-s4a-r1-e2e.log`).
- **Documentation:** `docs/ci-release.md` and `docs/quality.md` now state that verification independently enforces the target's required formats and target-prefixed names; the [F9 evidence record](../../verification/f9-linux-packages.md) carries the correction section and final counts.
- **Evidence class and limits:** deterministic script/renderer suites only for these fixes. The versioned-library case is synthetic and was not observed in a hosted AppImage extraction; the incomplete-set reproduction used synthetic artifact bytes. F9-01 (hosted artifact production) is still blocked on owner-authorised publication/dispatch, and installed-runtime rows remain pending.
- **Next:** commit and push this correction, open the pull request, read its Foundation/Security results, and then request the owner-authorised default-branch publication and `Package qualification` dispatch for F9-01. Do not create a tag/release or start S4-B/C work here.
